🎯 Best Ideas— verified dossiers + remaining picks: 12 verified + 0 proposals

Investment Desk verified dossiers — every figure fetched from live FMP data this week, confirmed by an Opus (top 3) or Sonnet verification analyst, then ranked by the CIO for 3–5yr risk-adjusted return. Proposals below dossiers carry the generator thesis only. MoS = margin of safety vs DCF. Click any card to expand.

CIO view: Zero BUYs survived this cycle, and that's a signal in itself, not a data-quality accident. Across all 13 names, Stage 2 verification found the same pattern repeatedly: generators built theses on flattering base effects (SAP's op-income 'doubling' off a depressed 2024 restructuring trough, TDW's fleet-reactivation snapback, WLKP's non-operating charge distortion), stale or internally-contradictory price targets (NVO's single degenerate $45 PT below spot, KLAC and QLYS both showing consensus PTs BELOW current price while claiming positive DCF margins of safety), or cited metrics that simply don't reconcile with GAAP (NRDS's claimed 8x P/E vs an actual ~14x, PTC's mis-cited analyst count and margin of safety). Layer on a cluster of insider/holder selling into strength -- QLYS's CEO offloading $6.5M across 8 transactions in the same week the stock rallied, NRDS's Topline Capital dumping ~1.96M shares right into a bullish article, KLAC's CEO selling repeatedly with zero offsetting buys -- and the picture is a market where sell-side and management behavior are quietly diverging from the bull narratives being pitched to us. This is consistent with a late-cycle, multiple-expansion-led tape: VIX at 16.4 and a positive but modest yield curve (0.31) suggest no imminent macro stress, yet richly-priced quality names (VRT at 88x trailing P/E, KLAC at 52x, QLYS with a broken margin-of-safety) are running well ahead of what consensus targets or fundamentals support. The Technology sector median PEG of 2.0 in today's brief confirms this isn't idiosyncratic -- it's sector-wide. When five of our best-screened, highest-conviction generator pitches (KLAC, PTC, QLYS, TDW, VRT) all got knocked down on valuation/data-integrity grounds, the read-through is that the easy money in this list has already been made and further upside now requires either a pullback to genuine margin of safety or a company-specific catalyst we don't yet have confirmed. Our posture this cycle is patience, not paralysis: several WATCH names have real, verified fundamental improvement (NICE's margins, PTC's growth, KLAC's process-control moat, VRT's margin expansion) sitting behind valuation or data gaps that a quarter of confirmation, or a modest pullback to the sell-side consensus PT (not the inflated generator DCFs), would resolve cleanly. We keep those on active watch with hard entry triggers and let the rest -- QLYS's broken valuation plus insider distribution, NVO's decelerating fundamentals with unusable PT data, 000660.KS's total lack of verifiable financials, ACM's stalled topline with an unresolved legal overhang, SAP's underwater DCF -- drop down the list until the picture clarifies.

#1PTCPTC Inc.WATCH — MEDIUM🥊 DEBATED⟳ ×4WATCHMoS +20%PEG 0.77▼
🥊 Deep Research Memo (bull/bear debated)
PTC screens as a classic 'cheap-quality' setup: 84% gross margin, ~21% ROIC, 13.9x trailing P/E, PEG 0.77, consensus PT +54%. The debate surfaces enough friction to keep it at WATCH: FY2025's 19.2% revenue growth coincided with a 67.1% jump in operating income and a ~$560M debt paydown in the same year -- the fingerprint of divestiture proceeds (Kepware/ThingWorx) hitting the P&L, not a sudden efficiency breakthrough after five years of 8% growth. No transcript or segment breakdown exists to confirm management's organic framing. Competitively, Siemens/Dassault/Autodesk all grew mid-to-high-single-digits in the same window -- a 19% PTC print sitting well above every peer, arriving the same year it monetized its faster-growing adjacencies, is circumstantial evidence the headline isn't like-for-like. Insiders sold exclusively, at prices 20-35% above today's spot. Net: quality metrics and margin of safety are real; growth acceleration is unverified and plausibly inorganic. WATCH/MEDIUM confirmed.

MOAT: moat — . Durability: Moderate-high in core PLM/CAD; IoT/ALM narrative undercut by divestiture news. Key threat:

Bull case: FY2025 revenue growth accelerated to 19.2% YoY, headline valuation (13.9x P/E, PEG 0.77) looks cheap, but acceleration is entangled with a disproportionate +67% operating income jump and news referencing a Kepware/ThingWorx divestiture that may distort the YoY comparison. Analyst count was mis-cited (15 vs actual 7) and DCF margin of safety understated (+24% cited vs +30.8% actual).

Bear case: Growth/margin mismatch suggests divestiture/mix effect not pure organic momentum; all insider activity in dataset is sales at $145-161, well above current $119.78, zero buying; no earnings transcript available to verify management's framing.

Competitors: PLM/CAD oligopoly against Siemens Teamcenter, Dassault ENOVIA/3DEXPERIENCE, Autodesk Fusion -- none of these grew organic revenue near 19% in the same window, making PTC's print an outlier worth independent verification, not face-value acceptance.

Capital allocation: C+ -- debt cut ~$560M (likely divestiture proceeds), but share count still grew ~0.3%/yr (dilution) and all insider activity is selling above current price.

Entry trigger: Confirm via 10-Q/8-K that growth is organic (not divestiture-driven); if confirmed mid-teens+ organic, upgrade to BUY.

What would change our mind: Upgrade to BUY: 10-Q/8-K segment disclosure showing organic growth still mid-to-high teens plus insider buying. Downgrade to AVOID: confirmation ex-divestiture growth reverts to 8-10%.

Price $124.90
#2NICENICE Ltd.WATCH — MEDIUM🥊 DEBATED⟳ ×9WATCHMoS +82%PEG 0.46▼
🥊 Deep Research Memo (bull/bear debated)
NICE sits at a genuine crossroads between statistically cheap value and a structurally decelerating growth story. The bull case is real (10.4x P/E, PEG 0.46, 10.6% FCF yield, net cash), but no earnings transcript exists to explain whether the 15%-to-7.7% deceleration is a blip or a trend -- that uncertainty is disqualifying for a BUY. The most damaging finding is a data-quality one: forward analyst estimates jump from 2025 straight to 2029-2030 implying an unsubstantiated ~54% single-year reacceleration, a modeling artifact not real consensus evidence. Given attractive valuation against confirmed, unexplained deceleration, WATCH/MEDIUM is confirmed -- not a value trap (balance sheet too strong), not a buy (growth denominator too uncertain).

MOAT: moat — . Durability: Moderate -- competitive category with agentic AI lowering barriers. Key threat:

Bull case: Trades cheap (10.4x P/E, PEG 0.46) with strong margins, but the kill risk flagged by the generator has already materialized: revenue growth decelerated from 15.0% (2024) to 7.7% (2025), confirmed in raw financials, and growth is lumpy, not 'consistent double-digit.' AWS/Sopra Steria news is real but unbooked PR. Downgraded from a prior desk run's BUY/MEDIUM.

Bear case: Real, confirmed deceleration 15%->7.7%; ROIC only 12%; FCF consistency only 60% of years; forward estimates imply reacceleration to levels not currently being delivered.

Competitors: Genesys, Five9, Salesforce Service Cloud, and Zoom CX all pressure NICE's CXone; Salesforce/Zoom's bundling of 'good enough' AI agents into platforms customers already own is the most structurally dangerous threat.

Capital allocation: B -- net cash flip and shrinking share count are owner-friendly, but 12% ROIC caps the grade below A.

Entry trigger: Confirm growth stabilizes/reaccelerates above ~10% YoY next quarter with disclosed AWS/Sopra Steria bookings.

What would change our mind: Upgrade to BUY requires a quarter showing growth back above 10% with quantified AWS/Sopra Steria bookings and a transcript explaining the deceleration. Downgrade to AVOID requires further deceleration below 7.7% or a disclosed large-account loss.

#3KLACKLA CorporationWATCH — MEDIUM🥊 DEBATED⟳ ×9WATCHMoS +60%PEG 2.65▼
🥊 Deep Research Memo (bull/bear debated)
KLA is a genuinely excellent franchise priced at a multiple assuming it compounds through the cycle rather than with it. FY25's 23.9% growth is a rebound off FY24's -6.5% decline -- the same up-down-up pattern that has defined KLA for a decade. No transcript or company-specific news confirms management frames FY26 as structurally different. CEO sold in three separate transactions over eight months with zero offsetting buys. We do not downgrade to AVOID -- the moat evidence is the strongest on the desk's list -- but confirm WATCH/MEDIUM: a quality compounder bought at a cyclical peak, not a mispriced bottleneck.

MOAT: moat — . Durability: High near-term (2-4yr) given multi-year fab requalification costs. Key threat:

Bull case: KLA's process-control/inspection franchise is a real, verified bottleneck in advanced-node/advanced-packaging manufacturing and the growth acceleration (revenue +23.9% YoY FY25 vs -6.5% FY24) is confirmed against filed numbers. But the generator's valuation framing is too rosy: Wall Street's own consensus PT ($209.44) sits 21-25% BELOW the current price ($266.19), directly contradicting the claimed 56% DCF margin of safety.

Bear case: 52.4x trailing P/E already prices in most of the upcycle; sell-side consensus itself implies 21-25% downside; FY24's -6.5% decline shows this is cyclical WFE-capex-driven; thin FCF yield (1.7%); zero insider buying, repeated CEO sales.

Competitors: Applied Materials, ASML, and Onto Innovation are all pushing into inspection/metrology adjacencies -- none show up in current margins, but a 52x multiple underwrites years of continued KLA dominance, not quarters.

Capital allocation: B -- shrinking share count and high ROIC show discipline, but thin FCF yield and repeated insider selling raise a soft flag.

Entry trigger: Upgrade to BUY if price pulls back toward/below ~$210 or FY26 guidance confirms durable growth.

What would change our mind: Upgrade to BUY on a pullback toward/below ~$210 consensus or FY26 commentary substantiating structural (not cyclical) growth. Downgrade to AVOID on China export escalation or margin compression from competitors.

#4NRDSNerdWallet, Inc.WATCH — MEDIUM🥊 DEBATED⟳ ×9WATCHMoS +76%PEG 0.37ᵀ▼
🥊 Deep Research Memo (bull/bear debated)
The bull thesis rests on a Seeking Alpha article that miscites both P/E (8x vs actual ~14x) and EV/EBITDA (3.5x vs actual ~5.0x) -- a source sloppy enough to misstate both headline multiples cannot be trusted on its qualitative narrative either, and that same source admits NRDS is growing 'despite declining traffic.' Topline Capital's largest sale tranche (919K shares, 47% of the total) was executed three days BEFORE the bullish article published, meaning a sophisticated holder was already exiting ahead of the retail narrative. Durable margins and a clean balance sheet argue against AVOID; the traffic-decline admission and pre-article insider selling argue against BUY. WATCH/MEDIUM confirmed.

MOAT: moat — . Durability: Weak-to-moderate -- limited consumer switching costs, dependent on search distribution not controlled by NerdWallet. Key threat:

Bull case: Genuine revenue growth acceleration (21.7% 2025 vs 14.7% 2024) and return to positive operating income, but the 'cheap at 8x' framing does not reconcile with GAAP figures -- actual implied P/E is ~14.0x. A major shareholder (Topline Capital) sold ~1.96M shares June 26-30, right into/after the bullish article the thesis leans on.

Bear case: P/E is ~14x not 8x as claimed; organic/SEO traffic is declining per the bull source itself; major holder distributed ~1.96M shares into the rally; only 1 sell-side analyst covers the name.

Competitors: Bankrate/Red Ventures, Credit Karma (Intuit-owned, structural data-moat NRDS lacks), and LendingTree all compete in a channel increasingly disintermediated by AI answer engines -- a sector-wide risk, not company-specific.

Capital allocation: B -- net-cash balance sheet and consistent buybacks are disciplined, but a single analyst and reliance on promotional-quality research are yellow flags.

Entry trigger: Reassess after Q2/Q3 2026 confirms traffic stabilization and no further insider/holder selling.

What would change our mind: Upgrade to BUY if traffic stabilizes and Topline's selling proves a one-time fund-level exit with no follow-on selling. Downgrade to AVOID if selling continues or traffic decline accelerates.

#5VRTVertiv HoldingsWATCH — MEDIUM🥊 DEBATED⟳ ×3WATCHMoS -1556%PEG 3.46▼
🥊 Deep Research Memo (bull/bear debated)
Vertiv's fundamentals are not in dispute: 27.7% growth, margin expansion 3.9%→18.5%, ~20% ROIC. But the stock swung from +9.1% to a 20% drawdown from highs within the same week -- the market is trading VRT as a leveraged AI-capex bet, not a steady industrial compounder, at ~88x P/E with ~2% FCF yield. Nothing in fresh news flow supports active share loss today; the selloff reads as sentiment repricing the whole AI-capex complex, not a Vertiv-specific break. CONFIRM WATCH/MEDIUM -- sharpened risk, no killer negative fact.

MOAT: moat — . Durability: Moderate -- engineering lock-in supports durability but Schneider sells comparable gear. Key threat:

Bull case: Real, accelerating fundamentals confirmed: revenue +27.7% YoY to $10.23B, operating margin expanded from 3.9% (2022) to 18.5% (2025). But stock is not cheap at ~88x trailing P/E; no company-specific catalyst identified for the July 1 selloff -- reads as broad AI-capex sentiment de-rating.

Bear case: ~88x trailing P/E and ~27.8x P/B price in near-flawless execution; wide consensus PT dispersion ($277-500); hyperscaler in-house cooling R&D and Schneider Electric competition are direct threats.

Competitors: Schneider Electric is a genuine scaled competitor, not a niche irritant; Eaton and nVent add flanking supply. Vertiv's Malaysia capacity expansion and Nvidia collaboration signal it is still winning incremental commitments today.

Capital allocation: B -- margin expansion and ~20% ROIC show discipline, but heavy M&A-driven portfolio building adds integration risk.

Entry trigger: Add on further weakness toward ~$277 or confirmation of a company-specific positive catalyst.

What would change our mind: BUY trigger: pullback toward $270-280 plus a confirmed positive catalyst. AVOID trigger: disclosed hyperscaler in-house deployment at scale or growth deceleration below ~20%.

💼 Goldman Sachs Small-Cap — 5 picks ▼
💼 Goldman Sachs Small-Cap
Small-cap hidden-gem scanner: $100M-$2B cap, 25%+ growth, thin coverage, insider ownership, moat, catalysts, red flags.
📖 Strategy details▼

Goldman Sachs pre-discovery screen — finding companies growing 25%+ in the $100M–$2B range before mainstream institutional flows arrive. Goal: tomorrow's $10B company at $500M today.

  • Size: $100M–$2B market cap — big enough to be legitimate, small enough to still multiply
  • Coverage: 0–5 analysts covering the stock (≥20 = already discovered)
  • Revenue growth: minimum 25% YoY for 3+ consecutive quarters
  • Insider ownership: founders/executives holding 15%+ of shares (skin in the game)
  • Structural tailwinds: AI, cybersecurity, energy transition, aging demographics, automation
  • Unit economics: improving gross margins and positive operating leverage (revenue growing faster than costs)
  • Balance sheet: 18+ months cash runway without needing profitability
  • Blindspot channels: spinoffs from S&P 500 companies, foreign listings overlooked by US investors, orphaned small-caps that fell below $500M and lost analyst coverage, post-bankruptcy equities with clean balance sheets
  • Near-term catalysts: specific re-rating events within 6–12 months (earnings beats, product launches, regulatory approvals)

🛒 Lynch Buy What You Know — 5 picks ▼
🛒 Lynch Buy What You Know
Everyday-observation → public-company mapping, classified into a Lynch category with a valuation sanity check.
📖 Strategy details▼

Peter Lynch's everyday-life observation framework. Simple, understandable consumer and workplace businesses growing 15–25%/yr at PEG <1.0. The best investments hide in plain sight — find them before Wall Street does.

  • Daily life inventory: products, apps, or services you're using more this year than last year
  • Emerging behaviour patterns: what friends, family, and colleagues are doing differently than 2 years ago
  • Workplace intelligence: what tools or software your company is buying or switching to
  • Kids' trends: products and brands teenagers are obsessed with that parents haven't noticed yet
  • Retail observation: which stores have lines, which brands are sold out, what everyone is talking about
  • Lynch category: classify each as Fast Grower (20%+ growth), Stalwart, Cyclical, Turnaround, or Asset Play
  • The 'so what' test: a popular product doesn't automatically make a great stock — always check valuation and fundamentals

📱 Camillo Social Arbitrage
Viral TikTok/Reddit/X/App-Store signals mapped to the public company capturing the economic benefit.
📖 Strategy details▼

Chris Camillo's social arbitrage approach — spotting consumer trends on social media months before Wall Street analysts notice, finding what's viral before it shows up in earnings reports.

  • Public company linkage: for each trend, identify the specific public company that captures the economic benefit

💯 Mayer 100-Bagger — 1 picks ▼
💯 Mayer 100-Bagger
Small cap + high ROIC + owner-operator + long runway — the 100-bagger checklist. May return zero picks.
📖 Strategy details▼

Christopher Mayer's 100-Bagger framework — the research methodology identifying stocks with potential to return 100x, based on patterns observed across 365 historical 100-baggers.

  • Small starting cap: ideally under $1B — 100x must be mathematically possible
  • Long runway: serves a market large enough to support decades of revenue growth
  • ROIC above 20%: Mayer's single most predictive metric — the engine of compounding
  • Earnings growth: 20%+ annual earnings growth sustained for many years
  • Owner-operator: CEO and management with 10%+ personal stock ownership
  • Reinvestment runway: profits redeployed into new growth at high returns (not paid out as dividends)
  • No dilution: the company isn't issuing large amounts of new shares that erode long-term returns
  • Twin engines test: can earnings grow 10x AND the P/E multiple also expand to compound into 100x?
  • Time horizon reality: 100-baggers typically take 20–25 years — patience is the edge

🚀 Wood Disruptive Innovation — 5 picks ▼
🚀 Wood Disruptive Innovation
AI/robotics/energy-storage/blockchain/genomics platforms, Wright's-Law reasoning, 5yr bull/base/bear scenarios.
📖 Strategy details▼

ARK Invest's disruptive innovation framework — identifying companies at the centre of major technological platforms that could produce exponential returns over the next decade via Wright's Law cost curves.

  • Five platforms: AI, robotics, energy storage, blockchain, and multiomics (genomics + proteomics)
  • Wright's Law: every doubling of production cuts costs predictably — ride the exponential adoption curve
  • Pure-plays only: 80%+ of revenue from the disruptive technology (not legacy businesses with a small AI division)
  • Convergence opportunities: companies at the intersection of 2+ platforms (AI + healthcare, AI + robotics)
  • TAM expansion: the total addressable market is itself growing as the technology matures
  • Network effects / data moats: companies that get stronger as they scale — more users, more data, better products
  • 5-year scenario: bull case (technology adopted as projected), base case, bear case (technology stalls)

🔢 Greenblatt Magic Formula — 10 picks ▼
🔢 Greenblatt Magic Formula
ROIC>20% + earnings yield>10% ranked screen, ex-financials/utilities/REITs. Top 10, not a curated shortlist.
📖 Strategy details▼

Joel Greenblatt's Magic Formula — the two-factor screen generating 30%+ annual returns over 20 years by finding companies that are simultaneously high-quality AND cheap.

  • ROIC above 20%: high-quality business generating strong returns on every dollar invested
  • Earnings yield above 10%: cheap relative to earnings (inverse of P/E) — the 'cheap' gate
  • Combined ranking: companies score best when BOTH metrics rank highly — quality + cheap together
  • Size filter: $50M–$10B to exclude illiquid micro-caps and already-covered mega-caps
  • Sector exclusions: skip financials, utilities, and REITs (different capital structures distort the formula)
  • Debt check: high ROIC shouldn't be mere financial leverage — verify balance sheet is clean
  • Earnings quality: free cash flow should match net income — flag accounting-driven earnings
  • Temporary vs permanent: is the stock cheap due to a fixable problem, or is the business structurally declining?

🎲 Pabrai Asymmetric Bet — 3 picks ▼
🎲 Pabrai Asymmetric Bet
3:1+ upside/downside bets with explicit downside protection — distress, special situations, hated industries.
📖 Strategy details▼

Mohnish Pabrai's Dhandho framework — combining LOW RISK (limited downside) with HIGH UNCERTAINTY (Wall Street can't value it) for asymmetric bets where heads I win big, tails I don't lose much.

  • Asymmetry ratio: upside potential ÷ downside risk must be 3:1 or better
  • Downside protection: what specifically limits the loss — asset value, cash, acquisition target, recurring revenue
  • Upside catalyst: the specific event that could drive 3–10x returns over 3–5 years
  • Temporary distress: markets treating a short-term problem (lawsuit, earnings miss, sector rotation) as permanent
  • Special situations: spinoffs, post-bankruptcy equities, rights offerings that create pricing inefficiencies
  • Hated industries: entire sectors abandoned by investors where survivors trade at distressed multiples
  • Margin of safety: gap between current price and conservative intrinsic value — the buffer against being wrong

🔄 Marks Second-Level — 5 picks ▼
🔄 Marks Second-Level
Consensus audit + second-level question — oversold/overbought extremes, narrative-vs-numbers gaps.
📖 Strategy details▼

Howard Marks' second-level thinking — first-level thinking gets average returns; second-level thinking ('the company is great BUT priced for perfection') finds the real opportunities.

  • Consensus audit: what is the mainstream view, and is it based on sound analysis or herd mentality?
  • Second-level question: what do I know or see that the consensus is missing?
  • Oversold opportunities: stocks down 50%+ where the negative narrative has become excessive vs fundamentals
  • Sentiment extremes: sectors at multi-year sentiment lows (rare to be wrong buying) or highs (rare to be right buying)
  • Narrative vs numbers gap: where does the market story ('this industry is dying') contradict the actual data?
  • Time horizon arbitrage: quarterly-focused Wall Street missing a multi-year thesis
  • Insider buying signal: executives aggressively buying when the market is selling — the strongest bullish insider indicator

🌀 Sleep Scale Economics Shared — 4 picks ▼
🌀 Sleep Scale Economics Shared
Businesses that share scale economics with customers via a reinforcing flywheel and long-term management.
📖 Strategy details▼

Nick Sleep's Scale Economics Shared framework — the methodology that identified Amazon, Costco, and Berkshire decades early, based on companies that share their scale advantages with customers to build a self-reinforcing flywheel.

  • Core test: does the company get MORE efficient and offer BETTER prices as it grows? (opposite of most businesses)
  • Customer obsession: willing to take short-term margin hits to give customers more value (Costco 15% margin discipline)
  • Flywheel: lower prices → more customers → more scale → lower costs → even lower prices (self-reinforcing loop)
  • Long-term thinking evidence: quarterly earnings suppressed for multi-year investment; founder letters emphasise decades
  • Customer lifetime value: expanding over time as the business creates more value per customer
  • Non-obvious moat: the advantage isn't patents or brand — it's a culture and business model the organisation embodies
  • Capital allocation: cash flow reinvested into high-return opportunities rather than buybacks at peak prices

🕳️ Burry Catalyst Deep Value — 5 picks ▼
🕳️ Burry Catalyst Deep Value
Hidden assets, sum-of-the-parts, activist/regulatory catalysts — each with an explicit timeline.
📖 Strategy details▼

Michael Burry's catalyst-driven deep value approach — finding mispriced assets where specific, identifiable catalysts will force the market to recognise hidden value within a defined timeframe.

  • Hidden assets: companies trading below the value of real estate, cash, or investments on the balance sheet
  • Sum-of-parts: conglomerates where individual divisions are worth more than the combined market cap
  • Activist catalyst: stocks where an activist could force a spinoff, sale, or restructuring to unlock value
  • Regulatory / legal catalysts: upcoming court cases or policy decisions that will materially reprice the stock
  • Balance sheet transformation: companies paying down debt aggressively — will re-rate once leverage drops below key thresholds
  • Accounting normalisation: one-time write-downs or unusual items temporarily depressing reported earnings
  • Catalyst timeline: each idea must have a specific catalyst with 6–18 month resolution — no 'cheap forever' open-ended theses

👁️ Insider & 13F Tracker — 5 picks ▼
👁️ Insider & 13F Tracker
Insider cluster buying + 13F smart-money overlap, top 5 as of today.
📖 Strategy details▼

Insider buying and 13F tracker — the only people with BOTH material information AND capital at stake. Cluster insider purchases and smart-money positioning are among the highest-conviction signals available.

  • Cluster buying: 3+ different insiders (CEO, CFO, board members) buying simultaneously within 30 days
  • Purchase size: buys above $100K from executives who already hold significant positions signal strongest conviction
  • First-time buyers: insiders making their first-ever open-market purchase often precede major positive developments
  • 13F smart money: Berkshire, Pershing Square, Third Point — recent initiations or meaningful position increases
  • Concentration signal: when a top fund puts 5%+ of their portfolio into one stock, that's conviction worth following
  • Combined signal: stocks showing BOTH insider buying AND smart-money accumulation — the highest-conviction setup
  • Red flags: cluster insider SELLING is equally informative — flag heavily selling insiders in holdings

🛰️ Wall Street Blindspot — 5 picks ▼
🛰️ Wall Street Blindspot
Coverage-gap hunting — foreign listings, spinoffs, orphaned small-caps, sin-stock discounts — as a prioritized workflow.
📖 Strategy details▼

Wall Street Blindspot framework — finding investment opportunities that major banks structurally cannot or will not cover: coverage gaps, orphaned names, stigmatised sectors, complex structures.

  • Coverage gap: companies with 0–5 sell-side analysts — institutions only cover above $2B, leaving quality names persistently mispriced
  • Orphaned mid-caps: companies that fell below $500M and lost coverage — often trading at deep discounts to intrinsic value
  • Spinoff aftermath: newly independent companies facing forced selling from index funds in the first 18 months
  • Sin-sector discounts: quality companies in gambling, tobacco, fossil fuels, or defence at persistent ESG mandate discounts
  • Post-bankruptcy emergence: restructured balance sheets institutional investors can't own due to mandate restrictions
  • IPO aftermath: quality companies 18–24 months post-IPO when lockup expiry creates selling pressure
  • Key signal: the discount reason is structural or temporary, not fundamental — the business is better than the price suggests

💼 Goldman Sachs Small-Cap▼ full report
AIRSHIP AI HOLDINGS (AISP) Growth: FY2025 revenue $15.3M against a $165.3M validated pipeline and $4.6M backlog implies a lumpy, contract-driven ramp. Coverage: Effectively uncovered, two nominal analysts. Insider Ownership: CEO Victor Huang holds ~12%, recent insider purchases (40,000 shares at $2.24 in March 2026). Tailwinds: AI-powered video surveillance/analytics for transportation, retail, government. Balance Sheet: $11.75M cash, minimal debt, but negative equity (-$7.1M). Moat: Patented video management/analytics with government-grade chain-of-custody. Catalysts: Pipeline conversion, Fortune 100 follow-ons, potential coverage uplisting. Red Flags: Negative stockholders' equity and market cap barely above its own pipeline size. Verdict: Medium conviction, size accordingly. RED CAT HOLDINGS (RCAT) Growth: Q1 2026 revenue surged 849% YoY to $15.5M; FY2025 revenue $40.7M, 2026 consensus ~$152M. Coverage: 6 analysts, Strong Buy, $22 PT (127% implied upside). Insider Ownership: 18.3%, founder/CEO Thompson ~10.8%. Tailwinds: Ukraine-driven rearmament in small FPV/loitering munitions; Blue UAS-approved Black Widow selected into Army programs. Unit Economics: Gross margin turned positive Q1 2026 after negative FY2025 (~3% margin) -- the key inflection to track. Balance Sheet: $167.9M cash vs $18.4M debt, fully funded 24+ month runway. Moat: One of the only Blue UAS-compliant, all-domestic-supply-chain small drone makers. Catalysts: Army program-of-record orders, additional Blue UAS certifications, NATO replication. Red Flags: Historical negative gross margin, director selling in June 2026. Verdict: High conviction, cleanest balance sheet and clearest structural tailwind. SENSEONICS HOLDINGS (SENS) Growth: Q1 2026 revenue +87% YoY; FY2025 revenue $35.3M, +57% from FY2024. Coverage: 4 analysts, Buy, median PT $11. Insider Ownership: unconfirmed current %, recent buying skew. Tailwinds: Long-term implantable CGM (Eversense, 365-day sensor) riding diabetes-prevalence wave. Unit Economics: FY2025 gross margin ~45%, up sharply from FY2024's ~2%. Balance Sheet: $40.5M cash vs $41.4M debt; equity recovered to positive $61.0M but burns ~$62.6M/yr. Moat: Regulatory/clinical-trial moat, 365-day duration protected by FDA data. Catalysts: Full Ascensia European commercial assumption, 2026 guidance $58-62M. Red Flags: Chronic dilution risk, another capital raise likely within 12 months. Verdict: Medium conviction, watch financing risk closely. VIVOS THERAPEUTICS (VVOS) Growth: Q1 2026 revenue +70% YoY; FY2025 revenue $17.4M, +16% (full-year undershoots the 25% bar but Q1 2026 is inflecting). Coverage: 5 nominal, 2 submitting estimates. 🛒 Lynch Buy What You Know▼ full report
Here's the thing about doing this job right: you don't start in a spreadsheet, you start in a parking lot. And lately the parking lot that's jammed is Five Below. Comps up 15% last quarter, net income jumped 41% year over year on revenue growth from $3.9B to $4.8B. FMP-verified, data_verified true. The stock trades around a 28-34x P/E depending on which earnings vintage, and net income is growing faster than that, so the growth-adjusted math isn't insane but it's not a screaming bargain either. HIGH conviction because the operating momentum is real, but size the position knowing you paid a full price. Next, the off-price trade -- Ross Dress for Less. Every retail trend piece says shoppers are trading down. Q1 2026 comps up 17%, but trailing twelve months settles into ~7.7% revenue growth and barely 2.6% net income growth. That's a well-run Stalwart having a good quarter, not a growth story. The stock's P/E sits around 34x on Stalwart-level growth -- paying growth-stock multiples for a steady compounder. MEDIUM conviction, watch for a pullback. Then there's the wholesale club trend -- Costco. Membership fee income up 13.6% to $1.355B, 82.1M paid households up 4.8%. Categorize it a Stalwart. Net income grew ~10%. The stock trades near a 47x trailing P/E, about 25% above its own ten-year median -- wonderful company, expensive ticket. MEDIUM conviction, mostly on the membership model, tempered hard by valuation. Worth a mention and a pass: the AI coding tool boom. Cursor is private, Anthropic is private, and the only public sliver is GitHub Copilot, itself a rounding error inside a $3.5 trillion Microsoft. You can't buy the trend directly -- LOW conviction, data-unverified placeholder, mostly to flag: this is the trap of buy-what-you-know investing. Last one, filed under boring but real: Walmart, riding value-shopping rotation and the GLP-1 effect quietly reshaping the grocery basket. Net income grew ~10-11%, solidly Stalwart territory. After Walmart's big multi-year re-rating, you're not getting a Fast Grower multiple deal here. LOW conviction as a fresh buy today. Bottom line ranking: Five Below first, growth real and multiple roughly keeps pace. Ross and Costco tie for a distant second -- great businesses, both Stalwarts wearing growth-stock price tags. Walmart and the AI-coding non-trade round out the list as things to know about, not necessarily buy this week.
📱 Camillo Social Arbitrage▼ full report
CROX -- Crocs, Inc. TikTok Shop's own shoe-category leaderboard named Crocs the #1 footwear brand on the platform. Crocs generated $52.4M trailing-12-month TikTok Shop revenue, up 210.8% YoY. HeyDude (sister brand) grew even faster at 277.65% YoY. Crocs launched a Valentine's Day 2026 TikTok microdrama watched by 10M+, turning a $6 Jibbitz accessory upsell into entertainment IP. Both brands sit under one roof, so TikTok Shop GMV converts directly into consolidated revenue. Risk: TikTok Shop revenue is still a small fraction of Crocs' >$4B total revenue, and Jibbitz/charm fatigue could reverse quickly. ELF -- e.l.f. Beauty, Inc. #EyesLipsFace remains the most-referenced viral case study in TikTok history: 5 million UGC videos, 7 billion cumulative views. Fiscal 2026 net sales grew 25% YoY overall, with individual quarters up 35-38% YoY, far outpacing mass beauty category growth. e.l.f. is the direct, sole public beneficiary -- price points make it the natural 'dupe' pick when prestige products go viral. Risk: growth deceleration signs (25% full-year vs 35-38% quarterly) mean any rotation to a new mass challenger brand hits the most premium-multiple name hardest. CELH -- Celsius Holdings, Inc. Alani Nu, built almost entirely on TikTok comparison/taste-test content, nearly doubled sales to ~$1.6B, prompting Celsius's $1.8B acquisition in Feb 2025. Celsius and Keurig Dr Pepper posted the fastest energy-drink sales growth of any major players. Celsius now owns two distinct viral-content engines. Risk: energy-drink virality is unusually flavor/packaging-cycle dependent; the $1.8B purchase price leaves little room for the growth rate to decelerate. DUOL -- Duolingo, Inc. Duo the Owl's TikTok persona (17M followers, 480.7M+ likes) has been sustained for years, not a single flash trend. Every viral hit drives measurable sign-up surges -- a repeatable, zero-paid-media acquisition channel. Risk: DUOL already trades at a rich multiple partly pricing in this advantage; the strategy is personnel-dependent (one specific hire, 18 months to build) and an AI-tutor competitor could erode the core value prop even while the mascot content keeps performing. FNKO -- Funko, Inc. TikTok unboxing content around KPop Demon Hunters and Bitty Pop! lines revived a brand TikTok's own 'Funko Pops are dying' discourse had written off. Q1 2026 beat across the board: revenue +5.3% YoY, core collectibles +17%, record 44% gross margin, European sales +20% YoY. Risk: management itself is guiding only flat-to-3% full-year growth despite the Q1 pop, implying the viral tailwind isn't expected to be durable; ~$20-40M annual tariff costs remain unresolved.

Long growth runway: PARTIAL PASS -- structural steel fabrication for data-center/energy construction has real tailwinds, record $561.1M backlog, but the addressable market is regional and commoditized. Score 5/10. 3. ROIC >15-20%: FAIL/MIXED -- FY2026 ROE ~14%, FY2026 gross margin collapsed from 31.6% to 23.1% on US tariffs, showing no pricing power to defend returns through a cost shock. Score 4/10. 4. 20%+ earnings growth: FAIL for FY2026 (revenue fell ~24%). Score 4/10. 5. Owner-operator 10%+ ownership: PASS -- Paschini family holds ~40% total/~85% voting. Score 9/10. 6. High-return reinvestment: WEAK -- capital-intensive steel fabrication, low structural incremental ROIC. Score 3/10. 7. Durable moat / no dilution: MIXED -- no dilution evident, but no brand/network/IP moat identifiable; commoditized inputs. Score 3/10 (moat) / 8/10 (dilution). TWIN ENGINES VERDICT: FAILS. Nothing in the current margin structure -- which just cracked under a tariff shock -- supports sustained 20%+ compounding, and there's no plausible narrative for multiple expansion from an already-modest industrial multiple. This is a reasonable value/special-situation idea but NOT a 100-bagger case. Tagging LOW conviction and flagging it likely doesn't belong on a Mayer100x buy list -- included only because it was the strongest name that survived verification. The framework explicitly permits shipping fewer or zero picks rather than padding the list. OVERALL 100-BAGGER PROBABILITY: Very Low across the screened set. FMP data coverage for Canadian small/micro caps is thin (three ticker formats failed to return statements), which materially limited verification rather than reflecting stock quality. Recommend a fresh screen with broader US micro-cap FMP coverage next cycle.
🚀 Wood Disruptive Innovation▼ full report
TSLA -- Tesla, Inc. (HIGH, FMP-verified) The clearest platform-convergence story in public markets: AI (FSD/Dojo/robotaxi), robotics (Optimus), and energy storage (Megapack/LFP) all riding the same Wright's-Law curve -- battery pack costs down ~90% since 2010, still compounding. Bear case (legacy auto margin compression, key-man risk) is real and is why this isn't a strict 80% pure-play, but the optionality in robotaxi/Optimus is the whole thesis. Bull (5yr): robotaxi scales to a multi-city network, Optimus ships hundreds of thousands of units, Megapack becomes a standalone multi-hundred-billion business -- target $900-1,400. Base: FSD scales slower, auto margins stabilize -- target $450-650. Bear: robotaxi setbacks, Optimus delayed years -- target $150-250. RKLB -- Rocket Lab USA, Inc. (MEDIUM, FMP-verified) Electron/Neutron reusable launch on its own Wright's-Law curve, mirroring SpaceX's curve a decade earlier. FY2025 revenue $602M (+38%), gross margin ~34% improving, still unprofitable (~$198M net loss) investing ahead of Neutron. Neutron unlocks national-security/constellation-replenishment contracts currently reserved for SpaceX/ULA. Bull (5yr): $220-320. Base: $120-180. Bear: $40-70. CRSP -- CRISPR Therapeutics AG (MEDIUM, FMP-verified) Casgevy is the first approved CRISPR gene-editing therapy -- proof the manufacturing/cost-per-patient curve for ex-vivo editing can fall the way sequencing costs fell. FY2025 revenue just $3.5M (down from $35M, largely milestone timing), continued heavy losses (~$582M). Pipeline extends into in vivo editing. Bull (5yr): $110-170. Base: $55-85. Bear: $20-35. RXRX -- Recursion Pharmaceuticals, Inc. (LOW, FMP-verified, price data incomplete) AI x multiomics convergence: phenomics/chemistry foundation-model platform generating proprietary imaging datasets, a genuine data-moat argument. FY2025 revenue $74.7M (+27%) but deeply loss-making (~$645M net loss). Bull: $18-30. Base: $6-10. Bear: $1.50-3. PATH -- UiPath Inc. (LOW, FMP-verified) RPA repositioning as an agentic-AI orchestration layer -- the transition is working, FY2026 revenue $1.61B (+13%) with GAAP profitability for the first time ($282M net income). Conviction LOW specifically because this is a reposition of an existing enterprise-software business, not a ground-up pure-play. Bull: $28-40. Base: $14-20. Bear: $6-9.
🔢 Greenblatt Magic Formula▼ full report
MAGIC FORMULA SCREEN -- Joel Greenblatt methodology (ROIC + Earnings Yield), $50M-$10B market cap, ex-financials/utilities/REITs. Screened 2026-07-02. In the current market, essentially NO name in this cap band clears BOTH ROIC >20% AND earnings yield >10% simultaneously -- quality compounders with high ROIC trade at low earnings yields (FIZZ, GNTX, HRB), while the cheapest earnings yields (SON) sit on low-ROIC or leverage-distorted businesses. 1. HRB (H&R Block) -- ROIC 38.8%, EY 14.5%, $5.0B mkt cap. Best combined score, but SANITY CHECK FAILS on leverage optics: aggressive buybacks pushed book equity negative, so ROIC is off a shrunken invested-capital base. Net debt/EBITDA 1.09x, FCF covers net income -- capital-structure engineering, not accounting fiction. 2. BAH (Booz Allen Hamilton) -- ROIC 17.6%, EY 11.7%. PASS quality check, net debt/EBITDA 2.8x manageable for contracted-revenue consultancy. 3. FIZZ (National Beverage) -- ROIC 27.4%, EY 6.5%. Best balance sheet, net cash, but market pays up for quality. 4. EEFT (Euronet Worldwide) -- ROIC 8.5%, EY 8.7%. Balanced, clean, neither leg clears threshold. 5. GNTX (Gentex Corp) -- ROIC 16.0%, EY 7.4%. Highest-quality balance sheet, income quality 6.15x. 6. CMC (Commercial Metals) -- ROIC 8.5%, EY 8.8%. Cyclical steel, watch construction slowdown risk. 7. ODC (Oil-Dri Corp) -- ROIC 15.4%, EY 5.5%. Net cash, but priced near 52-week high. 8. SON (Sonoco Products) -- ROIC 5.4%, EY 18.5% (highest raw yield). SANITY CHECK FAILS: negative FCF-to-equity (-$2.2B TTM), highest debt load in the top 10 -- looks like a value trap, lowest conviction. 9. HWKN (Hawkins Inc) -- ROIC 9.5%, EY 2.8%. Clean quality but too low a yield to be interesting. 10. NGVC (Natural Grocers) -- ROIC 9.0%, EY 6.5%. Smallest, most levered name in the top 10. OVERALL VERDICT: HRB and BAH are the closest true dual-factor hits; FIZZ and GNTX are highest-quality but too expensive; SON is cheapest but fails quality/leverage sanity check and should be a watch-list name, not a buy signal.
🎲 Pabrai Asymmetric Bet▼ full report

FY2026 delivered $272M net income and $1.32B EBITDA on $15.5B revenue. Debt has declined three straight years. Worst case: shares grind down another 25-35% to $11-13. Upside Catalyst: new CEO Michael Bender's back-to-basics turnaround; 2028-2029 EPS estimates already embed recovery to $1.50-1.58; real estate monetization could unlock $2-4B; recurring M&A/buyout speculation (Sycamore Partners eyed $65/share in 2022). Base case $25-30 over 3 years vs downside floor $12-13 gives ~3.5:1. Position sizing: MEDIUM conviction, size at 3-5%. OCCIDENTAL PETROLEUM (OXY) -- Downside Protection: Berkshire owns ~28% and holds warrants struck at $59.62-$62.50, a strong 'smart money floor.' Net debt fell from $24.97B to $21.97B after the OxyChem divestiture. EBITDA $11.67B on $21.6B revenue even at depressed oil prices. Worst case: retest $35-38. Base case $70-75 vs floor $36-38 gives ~3.3-4:1. Position sizing: HIGH conviction, size at 7-10%. ALIBABA GROUP HOLDING (BABA) -- Downside Protection: still 65-70% below 2020 highs in a hated China-ADR sector despite RMB 1.024T revenue, RMB 186.3B EBITDA, RMB 103.6B net income, all up YoY. Effectively net-cash-adjusted balance sheet relative to earnings power. Worst case: regulatory crackdown or delisting scare to $50-60. Base case $170-190 vs floor $55-60 gives ~4-4.5:1. Position sizing: MEDIUM conviction (geopolitical tail risk caps it below HIGH), size at 4-6%.
🔄 Marks Second-Level▼ full report
NIKE (NKE) -- First-Level Consensus: broken brand losing share to On/Hoka/New Balance, stuck in a multi-year reset. Is It Sound or Herd? Partially sound, but the depth of the decline has outrun the deterioration -- FY26 revenue is flat-to-slightly-up, the first sequential stabilization in years. Second-Level: new CEO Elliott Hill bought ~$2M at $42 in April, and Tim Cook -- with zero operational relationship to Nike -- personally bought 25,000 shares at $42.43 the same week. Supporting Signal: insider-behavior divergence; the market is reading Parker's neutral estate-planning gift as bearish while ignoring the genuinely bullish CEO/outside-CEO buying cluster. MOSAIC (MOS) -- First-Level: boring cyclical potash/phosphate miner down 37% over a year. Is It Sound or Herd? Largely herd -- FY25 revenue grew 8.4%, operating income nearly doubled, net income more than tripled, while the market anchors on the 2022 super-cycle peak instead of the improving 2024-2025 trend. Second-Level: why is the stock still selling off on random days with no catalyst, and why did the ENTIRE board transact on the identical date (May 28, 2026)? Supporting Signal: coordinated multi-insider buying plus a narrative-numbers gap. PAYPAL (PYPL) -- First-Level: poster child for 'disrupted disruptor' by Apple Pay/Shopify/BNPL/Block. Is It Sound or Herd? Mostly herd now -- revenue climbed $27.5B to $33.2B (2022-2025), net income more than doubled to $5.2B, margins expanding not compressing. Second-Level: why does a company with expanding margins trade at ~7x forward earnings implying permanent decline? Supporting Signal: CFO Jamie Miller made an open-market purchase in mid-June. ESTEE LAUDER (EL) -- First-Level: net loss of $1.13B in FY25, China/travel-retail demand permanently impaired. Is It Sound or Herd? Mixed, trending toward herd exhaustion -- the loss was driven substantially by non-cash impairment/restructuring on top of operating income that stayed roughly flat, and gross margin never cracked (~74%). Second-Level: is the market still pricing cascading decline when sentiment (Zacks Strong Buy addition, Reddit enthusiasm) has already flipped bullish ahead of fundamental confirmation? Supporting Signal: weakest of the five -- insider activity is only routine awards, no open-market purchases. WALT DISNEY (DIS) -- First-Level: bundled into the 'legacy media conglomerate being dismantled' bucket after Comcast's cable spinoff news. Is It Sound or Herd? Largely herd/stale -- revenue grew $82.7B to $94.4B, operating income nearly doubled, net income quadrupled over three years (FY22-FY25), yet the stock is down 13% YTD, lumped into the same breakup narrative Comcast just triggered. Second-Level: does Disney even need a breakup catalyst to re-rate if earnings power already tripled? Supporting Signal: narrative-vs-numbers gap; FMP consensus PT $137.83 vs ~$96 spot shows even the sell-side hasn't bought into the bearish breakup framing.
🌀 Sleep Scale Economics Shared▼ full report
COSTCO (COST) -- HIGH CONVICTION. The flywheel: Costco caps merchandise markups near 14-15% and lets scale compound into ever-lower prices. FY2025 revenue $275.2B against gross profit of only $35.3B (~12.8% margin), one of the thinnest in retail, yet operating income grew from $7.8B to $10.4B -- operating leverage from fixed-cost absorption and membership fees, not price hikes. Membership fee is the profit engine; merchandise sold near cost. Net margin ~2.9-3%, deliberately far below what market power could support. Culture-based moat: pre-paid membership psychology, ~90% renewal rates, wages set above retail norms explicitly framed as reducing turnover/shrink and shared with customers via price. AMAZON (AMZN) -- HIGH CONVICTION. Two engines: retail fulfillment density lowers unit shipping costs, funneled into lower prices/free shipping; AWS cuts prices 100+ times since launch even funding the flywheel elsewhere. Revenue $514B (2022) to $717B (2025), operating income recovered from $12.2B (2022 trough, even posted a net loss) to $80.0B (2025) -- the 2022 dip was Amazon absorbing fulfillment overbuild without raising prices, a deliberate near-term sacrifice. Bezos's 'Day 1' culture rejects short-term profit optimization; comp weighted toward long-vesting RSUs de-linked from quarterly EPS. CHIPOTLE (CMG) -- MEDIUM CONVICTION. Owns and operates nearly all restaurants (no mass franchising), keeping full unit economics and reinvesting scale gains into food quality rather than royalty extraction. Revenue $8.6B to $11.9B (2022-2025), gross margin roughly flat ~25-27% despite scale -- continued reinvestment into 'Food With Integrity' sourcing and crew wages rather than pure margin capture. Refusal to franchise is itself a long-term-thinking signal. MEDIUM not HIGH because gross margins haven't shown the secular expansion-into-price-cuts pattern as cleanly as the other three. RYANAIR (RYAAY) -- HIGH CONVICTION. Purest scale-economics-shared business outside Costco: fleet commonality, high-density seating, 25-minute turnarounds, secondary airports, ancillary unbundling all exist to compress unit cost per seat, passed through as Europe's lowest fares -- driving load factors, further diluting fixed costs. FY2026 revenue $15.65B, up from $10.78B (FY2023), ~45% growth in three years. O'Leary's comp is structured around long-dated multi-year performance hurdles, not annual bonuses. Net-cash position despite continuous large aircraft capex.
🕳️ Burry Catalyst Deep Value▼ full report
VF CORPORATION (VFC) -- HIGH CONVICTION. Net debt fell from $7.34B (FY23) to $4.16B (FY26), leverage ratio down to 3.1x from 5.1x -- crossing the threshold institutional investors use to re-rate from 'distressed' to 'investable.' Divestitures (Dickies, Supreme) did the heavy lifting; core Vans/Timberland/North Face is what's left, and Vans posted its first Americas DTC growth in four years. Catalyst: dividend already reinstated June 2026 ($0.09/share quarterly) -- a fired catalyst, not a hoped-for one. 6mo-1yr: Vans guided decline flipping to growth in H2 FY27. 3yr: further deleveraging toward sub-3x supports a re-rating to ~15-18x FCF. HASBRO, INC. (HAS) -- HIGH CONVICTION. FY2025 GAAP net income -$322M despite $1.058B operating income -- the gap is a one-time, non-cash $1.02B goodwill impairment in Consumer Products (tariff-driven). Strip that out: Wizards of the Coast/Digital Gaming revenue +44.7% to $2.2B with $1.0B+ segment operating profit at 46% margin -- a franchise with structurally higher multiples than the legacy toy business it's bundled with. Catalyst: 2026 guidance $1.4-1.45B adjusted EBITDA (6mo checkpoint); 1yr -- market re-rates toward SOTP separately valuing Wizards; 3yr -- structural separation or Wizards >50% of profit forcing a full re-rate. SIRIUS XM HOLDINGS INC. (SIRI) -- MEDIUM CONVICTION. Berkshire holds ~37% at a cost basis above market, continued buying through 2025-2026 signals floor-setting conviction. FY2025 revenue $8.56B, returned to profitability ($805M net income), net debt down from $11.18B to $9.61B. Catalyst timing risk: Liberty Media transaction structure contractually blocks meaningful buybacks until 2027 -- the real re-rating lever is pushed out past 1 year; nearer-term catalyst is subscriber stabilization and pricing tiers. PARAMOUNT SKYDANCE CORPORATION (PSKY) -- MEDIUM CONVICTION. DOJ approved the ~$81B Paramount Skydance/WBD combination June 12, 2026, funded via $47B new Class B shares. Sum-of-the-parts mispricing hiding inside a complex, still-settling merger structure -- studio + streaming + legacy cable historically valued in pieces. 6mo-1yr: integration execution, ad-tech reorganization already underway. 1-3yr: WBD's cable assets (old Discovery networks, potentially CNN) are a natural divestiture/spinoff candidate following WBD's own pre-acquisition playbook. THE KRAFT HEINZ COMPANY (KHC) -- LOW CONVICTION. FY2025 revenue $24.94B (down from $26.64B FY2023), net loss -$5.85B, net debt flat around $18.6B for three years -- no deleveraging progress. The Sept 2025 announced split into Global Taste Elevation Co. and North America Grocery Co. was a clean SOTP unlock, but was paused indefinitely Feb 11, 2026 following the net-loss year; new CEO instead reorganizing into three operating regions. Catalyst is now uncertain, real risk of abandonment.

🗺 Sector rotation

The 2026 rotation out of AI-driven mega-cap tech/communication services into value and real-asset sectors (financials, energy, industrials, materials) is well underway. Financials screen as the highest-conviction cheap sector alongside consumer cyclical. The crowded defensive trade (utilities, staples, real estate) now looks stretched -- richest PEGs paired with negative/thin FCF -- while tech faces genuine multiple compression after an unsustainable AI-scaling run. House view: rotate from mega-cap AI/defensive-bond-proxy exposure into financials, cyclicals, and selective industrials/energy value names.

🎯 Target Gap — fair value the market hasn't caught up to

The biggest gaps between a company's AI-derived fair value and its current price — large-cap compounders trading below their forward trajectory and under-covered names with a stated multi-year target the market hasn't re-rated to. Fair value is triangulated: forward revenue/earnings at the company's own growth rate × a justified multiple, cross-checked vs DCF, plus any stated management target. Hard numbers come from FMP; qualitative context from AI knowledge + web (labeled). Click a card for the full calculation. Research starting points — not price targets.

The macro backdrop is a study in contained but fragile calm: VIX at 16.4 and a positively sloped yield curve (+0.31) signal no imminent recession fear, yet the 10Y at 4.44% reflects a market still pricing 'higher for longer' after the Fed's June pivot away from cuts toward a possible hike. Sector valuations show the market has already bid up cyclical growth (Healthcare and Industrials trading at eye-watering PEGs on unsustainable revenue-growth comps, Technology at 2.0x PEG) while Financials and Consumer Cyclicals remain comparatively cheap. Over the next 1-3 months we expect continued two-way volatility rather than a clean directional trend. The Iran/Hormuz de-escalation and falling oil prices are disinflationary tailwinds, but they are offset by sticky core inflation (4.2%), a Fed that has removed its cut bias, and simmering AI-bubble anxiety that just produced a real global tech selloff in late June. Tariff policy remains a wildcard. Our base case is a choppy, rotation-driven market rather than a broad drawdown: quality/value names in Financials, Energy, and select Industrials with real FCF yield should outperform expensive, narrative-driven AI/growth names if bond yields stay elevated. We would use strength in mega-cap AI infrastructure to trim into rallies and favor names with genuine FCF over story stocks, keeping dry powder for a volatility spike around the July FOMC (July 28-29) or any Hormuz/Taiwan flare-up.

AI MARKET SYNOPSIS

2026-07-02 desk run: 4 generators + DeskGlobal + 12 Persona Desks + Tracked Portfolio surfaced 13 FMP-verified candidates (8 FMP-pool, 5 DeskGlobal open-universe). Stage 2 skeptical verification found ZERO outright BUYs this cycle -- 11 WATCH and 1 AVOID (QLYS), plus SK Hynix unverifiable. Debate on the 5 CIO best_ideas (PTC, NICE, KLAC, NRDS, VRT) confirmed all as WATCH/MEDIUM after adversarial review; none upgraded to BUY, none downgraded to AVOID. PM trimmed QLYS (AVOID-flagged, CEO sold into the rally) and PGY (decelerating growth + dilution) from the existing portfolio; no fresh buys. Sector Rotation flags Financial Services as the cheapest sector (PEG 1.0) amid a broad AI-capex-driven de-rating in Technology/Communication Services. Strategist house call: BALANCED.

🌍 GEOPOLITICAL & MACRO CONTEXT

House call: BALANCED. Low VIX and a non-inverted curve argue against a defensive crouch, and easing oil/Hormuz risk is a genuine tailwind, but a hawkish Fed that has dropped its cut bias, 4.2% inflation, tariff-driven stagflation risk, and visible AI-valuation fragility argue against full risk-on positioning. Stay invested with a tilt toward quality/value and real FCF generators, trim the most stretched high-PEG growth/AI-infrastructure exposure, and keep hedges/cash ahead of the July 28-29 FOMC and any Hormuz or Taiwan Strait escalation.

📐 Market Regime & Stock Style Signal
🛡️ DEFENSIVE — VOLATILITY RISK
High rates + elevated VIX or curve stress. Favor quality compounders, dividend payers, and cash-generative defensives. Reduce speculative exposure.
Current 10Y: 4.44% (elevated)
Sectors in this regime
TEND TO OUTPERFORM  ✓ Healthcare✓ Consumer Staples✓ Utilities✓ Banks (net interest margin)
HEADWINDS  ✗ High-beta cyclicals✗ Leveraged companies✗ Unprofitable growth
Stress environments reward quality balance sheets and predictable earnings.
Simple rule of thumb
